For binary balanced sequence length n, define s_i = (-1)^{a_i} (1 for 1? choose). Let's map. Let s_i = +1 for h_i=1, -1 for h_i=0. Sum s_i=0. Then a_i(1-a_{i+k}) = (1 - s_i)(1 + s_{i+k})/4? Improvement: Use pair coordinate descent to keep mass exactly. We can update two coordinates (j,k) moving mass δ: h_j' = h_j + δ, h_k'=h_k - δ (0≤δ≤). This preserves sum. For a given j,k, each F_e is quadratic in δ due to product of j and k if some lag connects them: F_e = B + δ a_j(1-h_k) - δ h_k? Let's derive more generally: Terms affected:
- If j and j+e: h_j(1-h_{j+e})
- If j-e and j: h_{j-e}(1-h_j)
- Similarly for k.
- Cross term if k = j+e or j = k+e: product h_j h_k appears and both vary: For lag e = k-j, term h_j(1-h_k) = h_j - h_j h_k. Under h_j+δ, h_k-δ: (h_j+δ)(1 - h_k + δ) = h_j(1-h_k) + δ(1-h_k) + δ h_j + δ^2? Let's compute: = h_j -h_j h_k + δ(1-h_k) + δ(h_j) + δ^2? (h_j+δ)(1-h_k+δ) = h_j(1-h_k)+δ(1-h_k)+δ h_j+δ^2. Quadratic +δ². Similarly h_k(1-h_j) = h_k - h_j h_k; under (h_k-δ)(1 - h_j - δ) = h_k(1-h_j) -δ(1-h_j) -δ h_k + δ². Quadratic positive. Other terms where one of them fixed are linear. So each active F_e is convex quadratic in δ (coefficient +1 for lag connecting j,k, else 0). Maximizing convex quadratics yields convex function of δ (max of convex) -> can be minimized by e.g. ternary/bisection subgradient. However δ range [max(-h_j,h_k-1), min(1-h_j,h_k)]. We can do golden/ternary because 1D convex. This could be powerful. For each pair, evaluate max over active lines with quadratic coefficient. But doing O(n) per pair and many pairs heavy.

After binary sequence, we can improve via binary local search:
- Use pair swaps: choose a 1 and a 0, swap (preserves count), i.e., h[a]=1->0, h[b]=0->1. For a swap, objective changes only for lags involving a or b, i.e., O(n) if using direct counts, and exact binary correlations can be updated O(n) by recomputing? We can evaluate full binary objective quickly via vectorized counts? For each trial, compute C via correlate O(n^2) direct maybe maybe okay but many trials? We can implement incremental exact update of full corr counts for binary swap to evaluate max faster.

Incremental binary correlation:
For binary vector h (0/1), define corr array length 2n-1 of counts (integers) equal np.correlate(h,1-h). If we flip one index j from 0 to1 or 1 to0, we can update corr in O(n): For each lag e from -(n-1) to (n-1), corr[e] changes by:
- Term where i=j: h_j(1-h_{j+e}) changes Δ_j * (1-h_{j+e}) if j+e in [0,n).
- Term where j+e=j: h_{j-e}(1-h_j) changes -h_{j-e} Δ_j *? h_{j-e}(1 - (h_j+Δ)) - h_{j-e}(1-h_j) = -h_{j-e}Δ.
So total Δ_e = Δ_j*(1-h_{j+e}) - Δ_j*h_{j-e} (where valid). For swap of a and b, changes linearly with Δ_a and Δ_b; since no cross if a,b not adjacent? But terms where e connects a,b: for a, Δ_a includes -h_b maybe old; for b includes 1-h_a; there's a quadratic? Wait flipping both simultaneously: If using old h, updating by sequential flips first then second might double count cross term? But evaluate increments as functions of Δ variables: For each pair variable (a,b), corr_e = B_e + U_e(Δ_a,Δ_b) where U includes cross if e=b-a: term h_a(1-h_b) changes with both. If we update by applying a then b using updated h, O(2n) but need keep max. Simpler: For local search, evaluate full correlate after swap using numpy for candidate; number trials maybe few thousand, n=160; each O(n^2)=25k operations, 1000 trials 25M, fine. Actually np.correlate direct 160^2 ~25k C; 1000=25M, <0.1s maybe. 100k=2.5B too high. But local search with improvements maybe need many. We can use incremental for speed later.

Binary local search details:
- Given h (0/1 int), balance = n/2.
- Objective for binary: compute max_corr = max np.correlate(h,1-h,'full') (integer). C=2/n*max_corr. We need minimize max_corr.
- Use pair swap moves: pick one 1 index and one 0 index, swap. Evaluate new max. To speed, we can compute candidate correlate by modifying current corr.

Direct count computation:
```
def binary_counts(h):
  n=len(h); C=np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=int); # index k+n-1
  for k in range(1,n):
    # positive shift k
    L=n-k
    C[n-1+k] += np.dot(h[:L], 1-h[k:]) # h_i(1-h_{i+k})
    # negative shift -k: ∑_{i=0}^{L-1} h_{i+k}(1-h_i)
    C[n-1-k] += np.dot(h[k:], 1-h[:L])? Actually h_{i+k}(1-h_i) = dot(h[k:], 1-h[:L]).
  C[n-1]=0
  return C
```
This is O(n^2) in Python loops with vector dot each k. n=160, 160 loops; fast. For full n=512, 512 loops; maybe okay for evaluating starts, not per trial.

- if i=j valid (0≤i<n and 0≤i+k<n): term h_j(1-h_{j+k}) change Δ*(1-h_{j+k}).
- if i+k=j (i=j-k valid): term h_{j-k}(1-h_j) change -h_{j-k}Δ.

Evaluation of swap using direct counts update:

Pseudo:
```
def flip_counts(C, h, j, delta):
    n=len(h)
    # self terms
    # k min = -j, max = n-1-j inclusive
    lo = -j
    hi = n - 1 - j  # inclusive
    # if lo <= hi:
    k_idx = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
    C[n-1 + k_idx] += delta * (1.0 - h[j + k_idx])
    # cross positive: k 1..j, i j-k
    if j > 0:
       ks = np.arange(1, j+1)
       C[n-1 + ks] -= h[j - ks] * delta
    # cross negative: k -1..-(n-1-j)
    r = n - 1 - j
    if r > 0:
       ks = np.arange(-1, -r-1, -1)
       C[n-1 + ks] -= h[j + 1:n] * delta
```
Need C dtype float or int? If updates with delta ±1 and h int, C integer. Use float to allow candidate h? For binary update int. Use `C` as np.int64 maybe operations with delta int. `C[i] += int(delta)*...`. `h` integer 0/1 for binary. Cross term `h[j - ks] * delta`; if delta -1, `+=` maybe subtract. Good.
Check boundaries: self terms for all shifts k such that i=j valid and i+k in [0,n-1]. condition 0≤k≤n-1-j? Since i=j, need j+k≥0 => k≥-j; j+k≤n-1 => k≤n-1-j. Inclusive. Good.
Cross positive: term i=j-k, valid i in [0,n-1]. If k>0, i<j. k range 1..j (since i>=0). Also need i+k=j<n ok. Good.
Cross negative: k<0, i=j-k>j. k range -1..-(n-1-j) because i≤n-1. Good.
Now `h` must be old before flip. If applying sequential, after first flip h updated before second? In formula for second, h includes first change. We can update h after applying flip: `h[j] += delta`. The C formula uses old h_other; if h_other was changed by previous flip, sequential works. For in-place revert, to revert we need know old values and deltas. Original h[j] old; after forward, h[j]=old+delta. To revert, apply flip with delta=-delta to current h (which is new); formula works. Good.

Trial swap:
```
def trial_swap(C,h,a,b): # a 1, b 0
    old_a=h[a]; old_b=h[b]
    flip_counts(C,h,a,-1); h[a]=0
    flip_counts(C,h,b,+1); h[b]=1
    new_max=int(C.max())
    # if reject, revert
    flip_counts(C,h,b,-1); h[b]=0
    flip_counts(C,h,a,+1); h[a]=1
    return new_max
```

Coordinate descent full:
```
def coord_descent(h_init, n, time_limit, max_iter_pass, use_sym=False):
    h = h_init.copy()
    dx=2.0/n
    ells = np.arange(-(n-1), n)
    best_c = compute_c5(h)
    best = h.copy()
    start=time.time()
    pass_count=0
    stalled=0
    while time.time()-start < time_limit:
       # recompute corr? np.correlate O(n^2) per pass. Could compute once per pass after updates, okay.
       corr = get_corr(h)
       for j in range(n):
           t = coord_opt_j(h,j,n,corr,ells)
           if t != h[j]: h[j]=t
       h=project_simplex(h,n) # preserve sum exactly, minimal L2
       c = compute_c5(h)
       if c < best_c - 1e-12:
           best_c=c; best=h.copy(); stalled=0
       else: stalled +=1
       if stalled>5: break
    return best,best_c
```
But `coord_opt_j` uses current corr from before update; after updating h_j, subsequent j in same pass use stale corr for terms involving previous updates. Previous code did same. For n=160, recompute each variable expensive? We can recompute corr after each block of e.g. 32 variables to update. But correlation O(n^2)=25k, similar to few coordinate line searches? It might improve. However coordinate line search uses corr for exact t; stale max may be off but still gradient direction. Recomputing per pass enough.

Continuous coordinate line search with direct corr:
```
def corr_direct(h):
   n=len(h); L=2*n-1; c=np.empty(L); one=1-h
   c[n-1]=np.dot(h,one)
   for e in range(1,n):
       Lh=n-e
       c[n-1+e]=np.dot(h[:Lh], one[e:])
       c[n-1-e]=np.dot(h[Lh:], one[:Lh])? For e negative: shift -e? Let e=-d: i=d..n-1: h[i] one[i-d], length n-d. h[Lh:] where Lh=n-d, one[:Lh]. Yes.
   return c
```
This computes both signs. It's O(n^2) Python loop n-1. For CD pass, maybe okay. For n=160, 159 dots; pass ~0.001? For n=512, 511 dots, pass maybe 0.01. Good. We can also use direct for binary counts as int.

Detailed `coord_opt_j`:
```
def coord_opt_j(h, j, n, corr, ells):
    m = len(corr)
    a = np.zeros(m)
    idx2 = j + ells   # for + (1 - h[j+e])
    valid2 = (idx2 >=0)&(idx2<n)
    if np.any(valid2): a[valid2] += 1.0 - h[idx2[valid2]]
    idx1 = j - ells   # for - h[j-e]
    valid1 = (idx1 >=0)&(idx1<n)
    if np.any(valid1): a[valid1] -= h[idx1[valid1]]
    b = corr - h[j]*a
    t0 = h[j]
    # check current
    vals = a*t0 + b
    mx = vals.max()
    active = vals >= mx - 1e-12
    sg = a[active]
    if sg.size ==0: return t0
    if sg.min() < -1e-12 and sg.max() > 1e-12: return t0 # min
    if sg.max() <= 1e-12:
       # function non-increasing at t0 -> optimum to right or flat. Evaluate at 1
       # if g(1) >= g(t0) and slope<=0? convex means if slope at t0 negative, decreasing to right until min maybe interior, not necessarily 1. Need binary.
       lo = t0; hi=1.0
    elif sg.min() >= 1e-12:
       lo=0.0; hi=t0
    else: return t0
    # if t0=0 or1 handle
    if lo==hi: return lo
    # bisection 25
    for _ in range(30):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       vals=a*mid+b
       mx=vals.max()
       active=vals>=mx-1e-12
       sg=np.mean(a[active]) # or median? Use mean; if 0 in interval enough.
       if sg > 1e-13:
          hi=mid
       elif sg < -1e-13:
          lo=mid
       else:
          break
    t=0.5*(lo+hi)
    # ensure not worse at bounds? compare g at t0, t,0,1?
    # Since convex, but our bisection using mean active slope okay. To be safe, evaluate candidates [t0,t,0,1] and pick min g plus maybe small regularization to reduce sum drift? 
```
But using mean active slope could be unstable. Better use subgradient interval [min active, max active]. To decide move:
- If max_active < -tol: g decreasing at mid => minimum to right -> lo=mid.
- If min_active > tol: g increasing -> hi=mid.
- Else optimal at mid -> break.
This uses any point in subgradient; correct.
```
amin=active.min(); amax=active.max()
if amax < -tol: lo=mid
elif amin > tol: hi=mid
else: break
```
Works.
After bisection, evaluate g(t) for t,0,1,t0 maybe. Also maybe if t changes sum drastically, we could prefer t0 if same value to preserve mass. We'll pick minimal g with a small penalty `1e-12*(t-t0)^2` or compare only.
```
cands = (t0,t,0.0,1.0)
g = [ (a*c+b).max() + 1e-12*(c-t0)^2 for c in cands]
return c[ min ]
```
But if t0 and t have same g, penalty keeps t0; good.

After coordinate updates, sum changes. Instead of `project_simplex` every pass, maybe do after each pass. `project_simplex` should minimize L2. Implement:
```
def project_simplex(v, target):
    # target float
    x = np.asarray(v,float)
    n=len(x)
    # clip maybe first? Standard for sum target with bounds [0,1]
    # binary search tau such that sum clip(x - tau,0,1)=target
    lo=-max(x); hi=1-min(x)
    for 40:
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       s=np.sum(np.clip(x-mid,0,1))
       if s > target: lo=mid else hi=mid
    return np.clip(x - 0.5*(lo+hi),0,1)
```

For a pair (i,j), move δ: h_i' = h_i + δ, h_j' = h_j - δ, δ in [max(-h_i, h_j-1), min(1-h_i, h_j)]. Need minimize g(δ)=max_e F_e(δ). Each F_e as function of δ. We can compute for active lines top? Exact evaluation O(active) per δ. We can do golden-section since g is max of affine or convex quadratics. Max of convex quadratics is convex? Each F_e may be convex quadratic in δ if the lag connects i and j (coefficient +1 for product term). If i,j not connected for e, affine (coefficient 0). Max of convex functions is convex. So 1D convex. We can do bisection on subgradient or ternary.

To compute F_e(δ) for a given e and pair:
Original contribution to F_e involves possibly i or j. We can derive linear/quadratic coefficients:
Let x=h_i, y=h_j. Variables x'=x+δ, y'=y-δ.
Each F_e is sum over terms; only terms involving i or j change. We can compute base B_e = F_e - contributions from i,j old. Then add new contributions using x',y' values. But many e; for each e, the changed contribution is:
- if i term: x'(1 - h_{i+e}) unless j = i+e then (1 - y') => x' - x'y'
- if j term: y'(1 - h_{j+e}) unless i = j+e then (1 - x') => y' - x'y'? Wait term where first variable is j: h_j(1-h_{j+e}).
- if i+e=j? That's included as j term? Let's do generic via old h and deltas? Maybe easier: compute new F_e for δ by evaluating full? But for line search need coefficients.

We can precompute for pair (i,j) arrays alpha, beta, gamma such that F_e(δ) = base_e + alpha*δ + beta*δ^2. Since only two variables, degree ≤2. For each e, contribution as polynomial in δ. We can derive by evaluating F_e at δ=0,0,-? Not.

Generating initial Markov symmetric balanced:
```
def generate_markov_binary(n, q, rng):
    m=n//2
    first=np.zeros(m,int)
    bit = rng.randint(2)
    for i in range(m):
       first[i]=bit
       if rng.rand() < (1-q): bit=1-bit
    # balance first to m//2 ones
    target = m//2
    s = first.sum()
    if s<target: flip zeros to ones; choose positions maybe random
    elif s>target: flip ones to zeros
    # mirror
    if n%2==0:
       h = np.concatenate((first, first[::-1]))
    else:
       # For odd, need n/2 integer impossible. Use n even only.
    return h
```
Target total n/2 means first sum = n/4 if even. For n=512, m=256 target128. Good. If n not divisible by4, target not integer; choose n multiples of4. Let's design candidate function `propose_pair(h, C, n, active_idx, rng, ones, zeros)`: returns (a,b) or None.
- If len(active_idx)==0: random pair.
- With prob 0.8 targeted active idx = active_idx[rng.randrange(len)], k=idx-(n-1).
   For tries up to 10:
     if k>0:
       if rng? Need i such that h[i]=1, h[i+k]=0. We can sample from ones: i=ones[rand]; if i+k<n and h[i+k]==0: return (i, i+k)
       (maybe if k large, many ones invalid i>=n-k; probability lower. We can sample from zeros with j=i+k: j=zeros[rand? but zeros list over all zeros, need i=j-k>=0; probability maybe. Try both.)
     elif k<0:
       d=-k; sample from zeros j; if j+d<n and h[j+d]==1: return (j+d, j) (a=one at j+d, b=zero at j)
       or sample from ones i; if i-d>=0 and h[i-d]==0: return (i,i-d)
   if no targeted, random pair:
       a=ones[rand], b=zeros[rand]; if a!=b.
Return.
Note: If k>0 and count C[idx] is number of such pairs, sampling from all ones works if count/ones not too small; if k near n, n-k small max count small; probability maybe small. Could use zeros if near boundary: for k>0, term i in 0..n-k-1. Sample zero j=i+k; i=j-k. If sample zeros, valid only j≥k. probability count/zeros. Both similar. We'll try both with random.

Potential issue: Swapping an active pair (one before zero for positive shift) may change the relation for the same shift to zero? Example k>0, i=1, i+k=0 -> swap to 0,1, term becomes 0, reducing C_e by1. For negative direction? Good.

Binary local search loop:
```
def improve_binary(h, time_limit, target_bound=None):
    h=h.astype(int)
    C=binary_counts(h)
    cur_max=int(C.max())
    best=h.copy(); best_max=cur_max
    ones=np.flatnonzero(h); zeros=np.flatnonzero(1-h)
    start=time.time(); it=0; no_improve=0
    while time.time()-start < time_limit:
       # maybe recompute active
       cur_max=int(C.max())
       old_act=int(np.count_nonzero(C>=cur_max))
       # if cur_max<best_max: update
       if cur_max==0: break
       for cand_iter in range(20):
           if time... break
           a,b=propose...
           if a is None: continue
           # ensure h[a]==1,h[b]==0
           if new swap maybe same? a!=b.
           # forward
           flip_counts(C,h,a,-1); h[a]=0
           flip_counts(C,h,b,+1); h[b]=1
           new_max=int(C.max())
           new_act=...
           accept=False
           if new_max < cur_max: accept=True
           elif new_max==cur_max:
               if new_act < old_act: accept=True
               elif rng.rand()<0.03: accept=True
           if accept:
               cur_max=new_max
               if new_max < best_max:
                   best_max=new_max; best=h.copy(); no_improve=0
                   # target early? continue
               else:
                   no_improve += 1
               # update lists dirty
               ones=np.flatnonzero(h); zeros=np.flatnonzero(1-h)  # simplify
               break? Maybe continue more candidates in same h? We'll break inner to recalc active.
           else:
               # revert
               flip_counts(C,h,b,-1); h[b]=0
               flip_counts(C,h,a,+1); h[a]=1
       if no_improve > 2000:
           # perturb
           if time...; if best exists reset to best? Or perturb current best.
           no_improve=0
    return best,best_max
```
Rebuilding lists every accept; for perturb after many no improve, we can reset current to best and then do random swaps e.g. 5-20 accepted regardless to escape, then continue. Or simply start new initial sequence from generator in phase.

We can set `max_tries_no_improve` around 5000 and then either perturb (random 10 swaps) or break to next start. Since multiple starts, breaking easier.

We also need `binary_counts` for h continuous? For initial binary only. Implement O(n^2) with vectorized loop:
```
def binary_counts(h):
   n=len(h); C=np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=int)
   one_minus=1-h
   C[n-1]=0
   for e in range(1,n):
      L=n-e
      C[n-1+e] += int(np.dot(h[:L].astype(int), one_minus[e:]))
      C[n-1-e] += int(np.dot(h[L:].astype(int), one_minus[:L]))  # for negative? Need h[L:] is indices e..n-1? one_minus[:L]=0..e-1. For shift -e: i=e..n-1 h[i] one_minus[i-e]. h[L:] length L, one_minus[:L]. yes.
   return C
```
For h int, dot int. `C[n-1]=0` always for binary. If h continuous not integer, use `corr_direct`.

`corr_direct` for continuous similar but floats.

Coordinate continuous candidate approach:
For each coordinate j, compute arrays a (for e≠0), b (for e≠0), and constants B0 for e=0. Then define function g(t):
- e0_val = corr[zero_idx] - h_j(1-h_j) + t*(1-t)  # since corr[zero] = B0 + h(1-h)
- other = a[t?] * t + b, but for e=0 a_e unused; set a[zero]=0,b[zero]=B0? Actually for e=0, if we add line a0? We'll treat separately.
- g = max(e0_val, max(other_line))
We need minimize g over [0,1]. Since e0 concave, g could have interior minima at boundaries or intersections. We can evaluate candidates:
   * 0,1,current
   * For each of top K lines (where current other lines near max), solve e0(t)=line(t) -> t^2 + (a_line -1)t + (line_const?)? Let's derive:
       e0 = B0 + t - t²
       line = A t + B

- `markov_first_half(m,q,rng)` as above. Flip to target count. Use rng integers/floats. For high q, use `rng.rand(m) < (1-q)`. To avoid slow Python for m=256 negligible.
```
def generate_binary_symmetric(n,q,rng):
   if n%2 !=0: n-=1 (but we choose even)
   m=n//2; target=m//2
   bit = rng.randint(0,2)
   first=np.zeros(m,dtype=int)
   for i in range(m):
      first[i]=bit
      if rng.rand() < 1-q: bit=1-bit
   # balance count in first
   diff=target-first.sum()
   if diff>0:
      zeros=np.flatnonzero(first==0)
      if len(zeros) < diff: # fallback global
      order=rng.permutation(len(zeros))[:diff]
      first[zeros[order]]=1
   elif diff<0:
      ones=np.flatnonzero(first==1)
      order=rng.permutation(len(ones))[: -diff]
      first[ones[order]]=0
   return np.concatenate((first, first[::-1])).copy()
```
If q high and diff flips many? count target exactly, distribution okay. But flipping random positions may create count but break long runs; unavoidable. Could balance by adding/removing runs at ends? Maybe more structure: Instead of random flips, adjust by flipping positions with minimal impact? Could sort by neighbor similarity. For first half, desired high autocorr; flipping a bit costs number of transitions? We can choose positions where bit different from most neighbors? To preserve runs, flipping an isolated bit maybe less? Actually reversing a single bit inside a run creates two transitions, bad; flipping a bit at run boundary preserves number? If sequence of runs, changing a bit may merge/split. But random flips few. Good.

```
def propose_binary_swap(h,C,n,rng,ones,zeros):
   maxv = int(C.max()) # passed maybe
   # active with max or max-1
   thresh = max(0, maxv-1)
   idxs = np.flatnonzero(C >= thresh)
   if len(idxs)==0:
       a=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; b=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]; return a,b
   kidx = idxs[rng.randint(len(idxs))]
   k = int(kidx-(n-1))
   # up to 15 attempts
   for _ in range(15):
       if k>0:
          # sample one
          if rng.rand()<0.5:
             i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
             j=i+k
             if 0<j<n and h[j]==0: return int(i),int(j)
          else:
             j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
             i=j-k
             if 0<=i<n and h[i]==1: return int(i),int(j)
       elif k<0:
          d=-k
          if rng.rand()<0.5:
             i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
             j=i-d
             if 0<=j<n and h[j]==0: return int(i),int(j)
          else:
             j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
             i=j+d
             if 0<=i<n and h[i]==1: return int(i),int(j)
   # random
   a=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; b=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
   return int(a),int(b)
```
Need ensure if `k=0` no active (C0=0). If `idxs` includes zero only? But maxv maybe0; handle.

Apply swap function:
```
def apply_swap(C,h,a,b): # a one ->0, b zero ->1
    flip_counts(C,h,a,-1); h[a]=0
    flip_counts(C,h,b,+1); h[b]=1
def revert_swap(C,h,a,b):
    flip_counts(C,h,b,-1); h[b]=0
    flip_counts(C,h,a,+1); h[a]=1
```
But after revert, C should restore exactly? With integer arithmetic yes. We can assert maybe not.

def coord_descent_real(h,n,time_limit, max_passes=300):
   h = final_feasible(h,n).copy()
   best_h=h.copy(); best_c=task_compute_c5(h)
   ells=np.arange(-(n-1),n)
   start=time.time()
   stall=0
   while time.time()-start < time_limit and stall<10:
       corr = corr_direct(h)  # direct array length 2n-1
       improved=False
       for j in range(n):
           t = coord_opt_real(h,j,n,corr,ells)
           if abs(t - h[j])>1e-12:
              h[j]=float(t); improved=True
       h = final_feasible(h,n)
       c = task_compute_c5(h)
       if c < best_c - 1e-12:
           best_c=c; best_h=h.copy(); stall=0
       else: stall+=1
    return best_h,best_c
```

Continuous coordinate candidate function:
```
def coord_opt_real(h,j,n,corr,ells):
    m=2*n-1; z=n-1
    # compute a,b for lines except? include e0? We'll set a_z=0,b0=corr[z]-h[j]*(1-h[j])
    a=np.zeros(m)
    idx=j+ells; valid & idx!=? include all
    a[valid]+=1-h[idx]
    idx=j-ells; valid
    a[valid]-=h[idx]
    # For e=0, both contributions sum 1-2h[j]; but we will treat e0 quadratic separately, set a_z=0 and b0=corr[z]-h[j]*(1-h[j])
    b=corr - h[j]*a
    b[z]=corr[z] - h[j]*(1-h[j])
    a[z]=0
    t0=h[j]
    # candidate list
    cands=[t0,0.0,1.0]
    # line-only minimizer via bisection [0,1]
    lo=0; hi=1
    for _ in range(30):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       vals=a*mid+b
       mx=vals.max()
       act=vals>=mx-1e-12
       amin=a[act].min(); amax=a[act].max()
       if amax < -1e-12: lo=mid
       elif amin > 1e-12: hi=mid
       else: break
    cands.append(0.5*(lo+hi))
    # intersections with top lines
    # compute current line values and e0
    vals = a*t0 + b
    e0_val = b[z] + t0*(1-t0)
    # top lines: use vals only, maybe those with vals > max(vals)-2*? Add top 20
    order=np.argpartition(vals, -20)[-20:]
    B0=b[z]
    for idx in order:
       A=a[idx]; B=b[idx]
       # solve t*(1-t)+B0 = A*t + B
       # t - t^2 + B0 - A t - B=0 => t^2 + (A-1)t + (B-B0)=0
       coeff = (A-1, B-B0)
       disc=(A-1)**2 -4*(B-B0)
       if disc>=0:
          r1=(-(A-1)-sqrt(disc))/2; r2=(... +)
          if 0<=r<=1 add
    # evaluate candidates
    def g(t):
       vals=a*t+b
       mx=vals.max()
       e0=B0 + t*(1-t)
       return max(mx,e0)
    best_t=t0; best_g = g(t0)
    for t in unique cands clipped:
       val=g(t)
       # prefer current if tie
       if val < best_g - 1e-12:
          best_g=val; best_t=t
       elif abs(val-best_g)<1e-12 and abs(t-t0)<abs(best_t-t0): best_t=t
    return best_t
```

def to_balanced_binary(h, n, rng):
    bits=(h>0.5).astype(int)
    # if h values continuous, choose n/2 largest as ones maybe better:
    order=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
    bits=np.zeros(n,int); bits[order[:n//2]]=1
    return bits

Let's write `flip_counts` robust:
```
def flip_counts(C, h, j, delta):
    n=len(h)
    base=n-1
    # self k where i=j:
    lo=-j
    hi=n-1-j
    if lo < 0:
       if hi >= 1:
          # update from max(1,lo) to hi
       # negative part
       ks=np.arange(lo,0)  # up to -1
       C[base+ks] += delta * (1 - h[j+ks])
    if hi >= 1:
       ks=np.arange(1, hi+1)
       C[base+ks] += delta * (1 - h[j+ks])
    # cross k where i=j-k:
    lo=-(n-1-j)
    hi=j
    if lo < 0:
       ks=np.arange(lo,0) # negative
       C[base+ks] -= delta * h[j-ks]
    if hi >=1:
       ks=np.arange(1,hi+1)
       C[base+ks] -= delta * h[j-ks]
```
Check j=0: self lo=0, no negative; hi=n-1 positive ks1..n-1. Cross lo=-(n-1), hi=0 negative ks only. Good.
j=n-1: self lo=-(n-1), hi=0 negative; cross lo=0, hi=n-1 positive. Good.
Need `h` is int array; `1 - h[indices]` int; `delta` int. `C` int. If using numpy slices with negative step `arange(lo,0)` where lo negative: e.g. lo=-3 -> [-3,-2,-1]. Good. For j+ks indices: j-3 etc nonnegative. Good.

def fractional_polish(h, n, time_limit, seed_rng):
    h = final_feasible(h,n).astype(float)
    c = task_compute_c5(h)
    best = h.copy(); best_c=c
    # build masks high low using thresholds dynamic
    start=time.time(); stall=0
    while time.time()-start < time_limit and stall<200:
       # compute corr direct to active shifts
       corr = corr_direct(h)
       maxc = corr.max()
       if not np.isfinite(maxc): break
       active = np.flatnonzero(corr >= maxc - 1e-8)  # includes top only
       improved=False
       # candidate budget
       for _ in range(50):
          if time... break
          # choose e
          idx = int(active[rng.randint(len(active))])
          k = idx - (n-1)
          a=b=None
          # sample for pair up to 30
          for _s in range(30):
             if k>0:
                i = int(rng.randint(n-k))
                u=h[i]; v=h[i+k]
                if u > v + 1e-6:
                   # move from i to i+k? To reduce term u(1-v), increase v and decrease i
                   a=i; b=i+k; dir=+1 # decrease a, increase b
                   break
                elif v > u + 1e-6:
                   # term for negative shift? For positive e, if v>u, term u(1-v) small; maybe move from b to a to reduce negative shift? We can still pair swap opposite?
                   a=i+k; b=i; dir=+1
                   break
             elif k<0:
                d=-k
                # valid i from d to n-1; choose i high, i+d low? term h_i(1-h_{i-d}); let a=high, b=low; decrease a increase b
                i = int(d + rng.randint(n-d))
                u=h[i]; v=h[i-d]
                if u > v + 1e-6:
                   a=i; b=i-d; break
                elif v > u +1e-6:
                   a=i-d; b=i; break
          if a is None:
             # random fractional high/low
             high_mask = h>0.55; low_mask=h<0.45
             if any: choose random from...
          # try moves with m=min(h[a],1-h[b])
          m = min(h[a], 1-h[b])
          if m <=1e-8: continue
          best_r=0; best_c_local=c
          for r in [0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.95]:
              move = r*m
              h[a] -= move; h[b] += move
              cn=task_compute_c5(h)
              if cn < best_c_local - 1e-12:
                  best_c_local=cn; best_r=move
          if best_r>0:
              # apply best_r (currently h is at last r; we need set to best)
              # We have overwritten h; easier store original values before loop

def slsqp_polish(h,n,max_iter):
   bounds=[(0,1)]*n

   def obj(x): return task_compute_c5(x)

   except: pass

`propose_binary_swap` needs `ones` and `zeros` arrays. To avoid passing, compute inside? We'll pass.
```
def propose_binary_swap(h, C, maxv, rng, ones, zeros):
    if len(ones)==0 or len(zeros)==0: return None
    n=len(h)
    # use active max or max-1
    thresh = max(0, maxv-1)
    idxs = np.flatnonzero(C >= thresh)
    if idxs.size==0:
        idxs = np.array([int(np.argmax(C))])
    # With prob 0.85 use active, else random
    if rng.rand() < 0.85:
       kidx = int(idxs[int(rng.randint(idxs.size))])
       k = kidx - (n-1)
       for _ in range(12):
          ...
    # fallback random
    a=int(ones[int(rng.randint(len(ones)))]); b=int(zeros[int(rng.randint(len(zeros)))])
    return a,b
```
For targeted negative k, sample high/low. If k=0, random. When sampling from ones, if near boundary invalid; attempts. Good.

`improve_binary`:
```
def improve_binary(h_initial, time_limit, rng):
    h = np.array(h_initial, dtype=int).copy()
    n=len(h)
    # ensure count
    if h.sum() != n//2: # balance by flipping?
       if h.sum() > n//2: ones... flip extras by random
    C=binary_counts(h)
    cur_mx, cur_cnt, cur_sec = binary_stats(C)
    best=h.copy(); best_mx=cur_mx
    start=time.time(); no_accept=0
    while time.time()-start < time_limit:
       # if current better
       if cur_mx < best_mx:
           best_mx=cur_mx; best=h.copy(); no_accept=0
       if cur_mx ==0: break
       # maybe early if best_mx*2/n <=0.375? skip
       ones=np.flatnonzero(h); zeros=np.flatnonzero(1-h)
       accepted=False
       # candidate budget: choose based on n
       budget = 8 if n>=500 else 16
       for _ in range(budget):
          if time.time()-start >= time_limit: break
          a,b = propose_binary_swap(h,C,cur_mx,rng,ones,zeros)
          if a is None or a==b: continue
          if h[a] !=1 or h[b]!=0:
             # try if reversed
             if h[a]==0 and h[b]==1: a,b=b,a
             else: continue
          apply_swap(C,h,a,b)
          new_mx=int(C.max())
          if new_mx < cur_mx:
              accept=True
          elif new_mx == cur_mx:
              new_cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(C >= new_mx))
              # compute new_sec
              less = C[C<new_mx]
              new_sec = int(less.max()) if less.size else -1
              if (new_cnt < cur_cnt) or (new_cnt == cur_cnt and new_sec < cur_sec) or rng.rand() < 0.10:
                  accept=True
              else:
                  accept=False
          else:
              accept=False
          if accept:
              cur_mx=new_mx
              if new_mx < best_mx:
                  best_mx=new_mx; best=h.copy()
              no_accept=0
              accepted=True
              break
          else:
              revert_swap(C,h,a,b)
       if not accepted:
          no_accept +=1
          if no_accept > 4000:
              # perturb: reset to best then 20 random swaps
              h=best.copy(); C=binary_counts(h)
              cur_mx,cur_cnt,cur_sec=binary_stats(C)
              ones=np.flatnonzero(h); zeros=np.flatnonzero(1-h)
              for _ in range(15):
                 a=int(ones[int(rng.randint(len(ones)))]); b=int(zeros[int(rng.randint(len(zeros)))])
                 if a!=b:
                    apply_swap(C,h,a,b)
              cur_mx=int(C.max())
              no_accept=0
    return best, best_mx
```
The reject path may leave C unchanged. Apply/revert exact. Need ensure `h` values after revert restored: We set h[a]=0,h[b]=1 in apply; revert sets h[b]=0,h[a]=1. Good.
Potential bug: If we accept, we break inner and outer top recomputes ones/zeros. cur_cnt/sec not updated but outer recompute stats at top? In code above after accept set cur_mx, but not cur_cnt/sec. At top of next while, we don't recompute stats unless we add. Need recompute stats after accept at top. We'll at start of while compute `cur_mx,cur_cnt,cur_sec=binary_stats(C)` instead of relying. That O(n) per outer, fine. So remove manual cur_cnt updates except used for old stats. In candidate equal, use current outer's cur_cnt/cur_sec. After accept, break; next while recomputes.

No_accept counter increments per outer with no accepted candidate; if 4000 outer no accept, perturb. If candidate budget 16 and each outer maybe few ms, 4000 may be long; time limit will break. Good.

Now let's think if `corr_direct` is correct and efficient. For continuous h:
```
def corr_direct(h):
    n=len(h); c=np.empty(2*n-1); one=1.0-h; base=n-1
    c[base]=np.dot(h,one)
    for e in range(1,n):
       L=n-e
       c[base+e]=np.dot(h[:L], one[e:])
       c[base-e]=np.dot(h[L:], one[:L])
    return c
```
For negative shift -e: i from e to n-1, h[i] one[i-e]. h[L:] where L=n-e, one[:L]. Correct. For e positive, i 0..n-e-1. Good.
This is O(n^2) Python loops; for n=512, 511 dots average length 256 -> ~131k multiplies per eval. Python overhead 511. `task_compute` uses np.correlate maybe C, faster? If np.correlate direct C, use that for final and fractional eval. But `fractional_polish` needs active shifts mapping; can use direct for active, but task for eval. To reduce overhead, in fractional we can compute `corr = np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')` to active? Order mismatch? If correlate order is reversed, active k mapping wrong. Better use `corr_direct` for active. For n=160, okay. For final eval, use task.

If `np.correlate` is faster than direct, we could implement `get_corr_fast` but mapping. Not necessary.

`task_compute_c5`:
```
def task_compute_c5(h):
    n=len(h)
    if n==0: return 1.0
    corr=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode="full")
    return float(np.max(corr))* (2.0/n)
```
This matches task. For binary direct we use internal C. If there is any mismatch, final c may differ; okay.

`project_simplex` with binary? For continuous final. For binary h, project not needed. For continuous h, sum target n/2. Implementation:
```
def project_simplex(v, target):
   x=np.asarray(v,dtype=float).copy()
   n=len(x)
   if n==0: return x
   target=float(target)
   if target<=0: return np.zeros(n)
   if target>=n: return np.ones(n)
   # binary search tau
   lo=-np.max(x)-1.0; hi=1.0-np.min(x)+1.0
   # ensure sum at lo/hi
   for _ in range(60):
      mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
      y=x-mid
      y[y<0]=0.0; y[y>1]=1.0
      s=float(np.sum(y))
      if s > target:
         lo=mid
      else:
         hi=mid
   y=x-0.5*(lo+hi); ...
   # adjust tiny
   diff=target - float(np.sum(y))
   if diff>0:
       idx=np.where(y<1.0)[0]
       # distribute to idx? simple add to first free? but that may cause nonuniform; okay.
       for i in idx:
          add=min(diff,1-y[i]); y[i]+=add; diff-=add
          if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
   else:
       idx=np.where(y>0.0)[0]
       for i in idx:
          sub=min(-diff,y[i]); y[i]-=sub; diff+=sub
          if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
   return y
```
If target=n/2. For integer binary, projection of [0,1] sum already target returns same (within). But if not exact due to flips, we can final.
Need no nested loops? Top-level loops fine.

`fractional_polish` details:
```
def fractional_polish(h, n, time_limit, rng):
    h = feasible(h, n).astype(float)
    c = task_compute_c5(h)
    best = h.copy(); best_c = c
    start=time.time(); stall=0
    while time.time()-start < time_limit and stall < 150:
        if c < best_c:
            best_c=c; best=h.copy(); stall=0
        corr=corr_direct(h)
        maxv=float(corr.max())
        if not np.isfinite(maxv): break
        active=np.flatnonzero(corr >= maxv - 1e-8)
        if active.size==0: active=np.array([int(np.argmax(corr))])
        high=np.flatnonzero(h > 0.55)
        low=np.flatnonzero(h < 0.45)
        improved=False
        for _ in range(25):
            if time.time()-start >= time_limit: break
            idx=int(active[int(rng.randint(active.size))])
            d=abs(idx - (n-1))
            a=b=None
            # try shift +d and -d
            if d>0:
                for _s in range(30):
                    if rng.rand()<0.5:
                       i=int(rng.randint(n-d))
                       u=h[i]; v=h[i+d]
                       if u > v + 1e-5:
                          a=i; b=i+d; break
                       elif v > u + 1e-5:
                          a=i+d; b=i; break
                    else:
                       i=d+int(rng.randint(n-d))
                       u=h[i]; v=h[i-d]
                       if u > v + 1e-5:
                          a=i; b=i-d; break
                       elif v > u + 1e-5:
                          a=i-d; b=i; break
            if a is None:
                if high.size and low.size:
                   a=int(high[int(rng.randint(high.size))]); b=int(low[int(rng.randint(low.size))])
                else: continue
            if a is None or b is None or a==b: continue
            x=h[a]; y=h[b]
            if x <= y + 1e-6:
                # if not high to low, could swap orientation: if y>x move from b to a? But we set a high? In random, high/low. In targeted, we broke when one > other and set a high.
                continue
            m=max(0.0, min(x, 1.0-y))
            if m <= 1e-8: continue
            x0=x; y0=y
            best_local=c; best_move=0.0
            for r in (0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.95):
                mv=r*m
                if mv<=1e-8: continue
                h[a]=x0-mv; h[b]=y0+mv
                cn=task_compute_c5(h)
                if cn < best_local - 1e-12:
                    best_local=cn; best_move=mv
            if best_move>0:
                h[a]=x0-best_move; h[b]=y0+best_move
                c=best_local
                if c < best_c - 1e-12:
                    best_c=c; best=h.copy()
                improved=True
                stall=0
                break
            else:
                h[a]=x0; h[b]=y0
        if not improved:
            stall +=1
    h=feasible(h,n)
    c=task_compute_c5(h)
    if c < best_c:
       best_c=c; best=h.copy()
    return best, best_c
```
If targeted sets a low? We only set when u>v or v>u and a high,b low. Good. If no high/low random, but binary sequence has many high/low; fractional after some values maybe.
`corr_direct` for n=512 in each outer can be somewhat slow; outer maybe 20 per polish, okay.
R values include 0.95. If move makes a small, b close1. Good.

top1h=None; top1c=1.0; top2h=None; top2c=1.0
for cand in candidates:

   if c<top1c: top2=top1; top1=...

```

       if cand_list[j][1] < bestval:

```

Pseudo `run`:
```
def run(...):
   start_all=time.time()
   rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
   def? no.
   parent=None
   try:
      parent=np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).ravel()
      if parent.size==0: parent=None
   except Exception: parent=None
   best_h=None; best_c=1.0
   cand=[]
   # helper inline code to update? can't function. We'll manually.
   if parent is not None:
      hp=feasible(parent, parent.size)
      cp=task_compute_c5(hp)
      best_h=hp; best_c=cp; cand.append((hp, cp, hp.size))
   if parent is not None and remaining>60 and parent.size%2==0:
      pb=to_balanced_binary(parent,parent.size,rng)
      limit=min(80, budget_s-(time.time()-start_all)-20)
      hib,mb=improve_binary(pb,limit,rng)
      htmp=hib.astype(float); ct=task_compute_c5(htmp)
      if ct < best_c: ... cand append
   n_list = [160,240,320,384,512]
   if parent is not None: n_list.insert(0,parent.size)
   # unique
   n_uniq=[]
   for x in n_list:
      if x not in n_uniq: n_uniq.append(x)
   for n in n_uniq:
      if budget_s - (time.time()-start_all) < 60: break
      if n % 2 !=0: continue
      # n maybe >600? if parent large, maybe too slow; cap if n>640 use continuous? But binary for 2000 too slow. If parent size 160 ok. If parent size larger skip binary for >800 maybe; use fractional only.
      if n > 800: continue
      if n <= 200: attempts=5; per=30
      elif n <= 320: attempts=4; per=25
      else: attempts=3; per=20
      for att in range(attempts):
         rem = budget_s - (time.time()-start_all)
         if rem < per+10: break
         hstart=generate_start(n,att,rng)
         limit=min(per, rem-10)
         hib,mb=improve_binary(hstart,limit,rng)
         # if mb*2/n looks good, evaluate
         cdirect=2.0*mb/n
         if cdirect < best_c + 0.005 or cdirect < 0.385:
            htmp=hib.astype(float)
            ct=task_compute_c5(htmp)
            if ct < best_c:
               best_h=htmp; best_c=ct
            if ct < 0.39:
               cand.append((htmp,ct,n))
   # sort cand
   ordered=[]
   while cand:
      bi=-1; bv=1e9
      for i in range(len(cand)):
         if cand[i][1] < bv:
            bv=cand[i][1]; bi=i
      if bi<0: break
      ordered.append(cand.pop(bi))
   # polish top up to 2 distinct n
   polished_n=[]
   for idx in range(min(2,len(ordered))):
      h,d,c,n=ordered[idx]
      if n in polished_n: continue
      rem = budget_s - (time.time()-start_all)
      if rem < 40: break
      if n <=200: lim=min(120, rem-20)
      elif n <=400: lim=min(120, rem-20)
      else: lim=min(100, rem-20)
      hf,cf=fractional_polish(h.copy(), n, lim, rng)
      polished_n.append(n)
      if cf < best_c:
         best_h=hf; best_c=cf
      cand.append((hf,cf,n))
   # if still above 0.38085, do a couple focused searches
   for extra in range(6):
      rem = budget_s - (time.time()-start_all)
      if rem < 40 or best_c <= 0.3800: break
      n = 512 if rem>120 else 320
      q = 0.99 if extra%2==0 else 0.975
      hstart=markov_binary(n,q,rng)
      limit=min(60, rem-20)
      hib,mb=improve_binary(hstart,limit,rng)
      htmp=hib.astype(float); ct=task_compute_c5(htmp)
      if ct < best_c: best_h=htmp; best_c=ct
      # optionally quick fractional
      rem=...
      if rem>50 and ct < 0.382:
          hf2,cf2=fractional_polish(htmp,n,min(50,rem-20),rng)
          if cf2<best_c: best_h=hf2; best_c=cf2
   # final feasible evaluate
   if best_h is None:
      hfallback=feasible(markov_binary(160,0.95,np.random.RandomState(1)),160)
      best_h=...? Wait markov returns int; feasible with target 80, c=task.
   best_h=feasible(best_h, len(best_h))
   best_c=task_compute_c5(best_h)
   return (best_h, best_c, len(best_h))
```

Now, possible bug in `binary_stats`: C is int, `less = C[C < mx]` if mx=0 less empty. `sec=-1`.

`propose_binary_swap`: It computes `idxs = np.flatnonzero(C >= thresh)` every candidate. If maxv small, includes e=0? C[zero]=0; if maxv=0, break. If maxv=1, thresh=0 includes zero and all, may choose e=0 (d=0) if idx base? In binary, C[zero]=0, so if maxv=1, thresh=0, active includes many zeros? We only consider idxs with C>=0 includes all, could choose zero shift, k=0, then fallback random. That's inefficient. Better use `thresh = max(0, maxv)`? We want max and max-1, but if maxv>0, choose `maxv-1` not 0 if maxv=1? `max(0, maxv-1)=0`, includes all. We should choose only shifts with count >= maxv - 1 and >0? Or threshold = maxv if maxv <=1? If maxv=1, all nonzero shifts? C zero includes 0. We can filter `C > 0`. For active, do `idxs = np.flatnonzero((C >= max(0,maxv-1)) & (C > 0))`. If none, choose argmax (which may be zero if all zero). If maxv=0 break. Good.
```
thresh = maxv - 1

```python
import numpy as np
import time


def task_compute_c5(h):
    n = int(len(h))
    if n <= 0:
        return 1.0
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(corr)) * (2.0 / n)


def corr_direct(h):
    n = int(len(h))
    c = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    one = 1.0 - h
    base = n - 1
    c[base] = float(np.dot(h, one))
    for e in range(1, n):
        L = n - e
        c[base + e] = float(np.dot(h[:L], one[e:]))
        c[base - e] = float(np.dot(h[L:], one[:L]))
    return c


def binary_counts(h):
    n = int(len(h))
    C = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.int64)
    one = 1 - h
    base = n - 1
    C[base] = 0
    for e in range(1, n):
        L = n - e
        C[base + e] = int(np.dot(h[:L], one[e:]))
        C[base - e] = int(np.dot(h[L:], one[:L]))
    return C


def project_simplex(v, target):
    x = np.asarray(v, dtype=float).copy()
    n = int(len(x))
    target = float(target)
    if n == 0:
        return x
    if target <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if target >= float(n):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=float)

    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if abs(s - target) < 1e-12 and x.min() >= -1e-12 and x.max() <= 1.0 + 1e-12:
        return np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)

    lo = -1.0
    hi = 1.0
    for _ in range(70):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        y = x - mid
        np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0, out=y)
        sy = float(np.sum(y))
        if sy > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    y = x - 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0, out=y)

    diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
    if diff > 0.0:
        idxs = np.flatnonzero(y < 1.0)
        for i in idxs:
            add = min(diff, 1.0 - y[i])
            if add <= 0.0:
                continue
            y[i] += add
            diff -= add
            if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
                break
    elif diff < 0.0:
        idxs = np.flatnonzero(y > 0.0)
        for i in idxs:
            sub = min(-diff, y[i])
            if sub <= 0.0:
                continue
            y[i] -= sub
            diff += sub
            if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
                break
    return y


def feasible(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).ravel()
    if len(h) != n:
        h = h[:n] if len(h) > n else np.pad(h, (0, n - len(h)))
    target = 0.5 * n
    s = float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(s - target) < 1e-10 and h.min() >= -1e-12 and h.max() <= 1.0 + 1e-12:
        return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    return project_simplex(h, target)


def to_balanced_binary(h, n, rng):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).ravel()
    if n % 2 != 0:
        n = n - 1
    bits = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    k = n // 2
    if k <= 0:
        return bits
    order = np.argsort(h[:n])[::-1][:k]
    bits[order] = 1
    return bits


def markov_binary(n, q, rng):
    if n % 2 != 0:
        n = n - 1
    m = n // 2
    target = m // 2
    first = np.empty(m, dtype=np.int64)
    trans = 1.0 - q
    if trans < 0.0:
        trans = 0.0
    if trans > 1.0:
        trans = 1.0
    bit = 0 if rng.rand() < 0.5 else 1
    for i in range(m):
        first[i] = bit
        if rng.rand() < trans:
            bit ^= 1

    cnt = int(np.sum(first))
    diff = target - cnt
    if diff > 0:
        zeros = np.flatnonzero(first == 0)
        if zeros.size >= diff:
            take = rng.permutation(zeros.size)[:diff]
            first[zeros[take]] = 1
    elif diff < 0:
        ones = np.flatnonzero(first == 1)
        take = rng.permutation(ones.size)[: -diff]
        if take.size == -diff:
            first[ones[take]] = 0

    out = np.concatenate((first, first[::-1])).astype(np.int64)
    if out.size == n:
        return out
    return out[:n]


def random_balanced_binary(n, rng):
    if n % 2 != 0:
        n = n - 1
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    k = n // 2
    if k <= 0:
        return h
    idx = rng.choice(n, size=k, replace=False)
    h[idx] = 1
    return h


def periodic_binary(n, rng):
    if n % 2 != 0:
        n = n - 1
    if n % 4 == 0:
        base = np.tile(np.array([1, 1, 0, 0], dtype=np.int64), n // 4)
    else:
        base = np.tile(np.array([1, 0], dtype=np.int64), n // 2)
    rot = int(rng.randint(n)) if n > 0 else 0
    return np.roll(base, rot).astype(np.int64)


def generate_start(n, att, rng):
    a = att % 7
    if a == 0:
        return markov_binary(n, 0.950, rng)
    if a == 1:
        return markov_binary(n, 0.905, rng)
    if a == 2:
        return markov_binary(n, 0.980, rng)
    if a == 3:
        return markov_binary(n, 0.930, rng)
    if a == 4:
        return markov_binary(n, 0.990, rng)
    if a == 5:
        return random_balanced_binary(n, rng)
    return periodic_binary(n, rng)


def flip_counts(C, h, j, delta):
    n = int(len(h))
    base = n - 1

    lo = -j
    hi = n - 1 - j
    if lo < 0:
        ks = np.arange(lo, 0)
        C[base + ks] += delta * (1 - h[j + ks])
    if hi >= 1:
        ks = np.arange(1, hi + 1)
        C[base + ks] += delta * (1 - h[j + ks])

    lo = -(n - 1 - j)
    hi = j
    if lo < 0:
        ks = np.arange(lo, 0)
        C[base + ks] -= delta * h[j - ks]
    if hi >= 1:
        ks = np.arange(1, hi + 1)
        C[base + ks] -= delta * h[j - ks]


def apply_swap(C, h, a, b):
    flip_counts(C, h, a, -1)
    h[a] = 0
    flip_counts(C, h, b, 1)
    h[b] = 1


def revert_swap(C, h, a, b):
    flip_counts(C, h, b, -1)
    h[b] = 0
    flip_counts(C, h, a, 1)
    h[a] = 1


def binary_stats(C):
    mx = int(C.max())
    cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(C >= mx))
    less = C[C < mx]
    sec = int(less.max()) if less.size else -1
    return mx, cnt, sec


def propose_binary_swap(h, C, maxv, rng, ones, zeros):
    if ones.size == 0 or zeros.size == 0:
        return None
    n = int(len(h))

    thresh = maxv - 1
    if thresh < 0:
        thresh = 0
    idxs = np.flatnonzero(C >= thresh)
    if idxs.size:
        idxs = idxs[C[idxs] > 0]
    if idxs.size == 0:
        idxs = np.array([int(np.argmax(C))])

    if rng.rand() < 0.85:
        kidx = int(idxs[int(rng.randint(idxs.size))])
        k = kidx - (n - 1)
        if abs(k) > 0:
            for _ in range(14):
                if k > 0:
                    if rng.rand() < 0.5:
                        i = int(ones[int(rng.randint(ones.size))])
                        j = i + k
                        if j < n and h[j] == 0:
                            return i, j
                    else:
                        j = int(zeros[int(rng.randint(zeros.size))])
                        i = j - k
                        if i >= 0 and i < n and h[i] == 1:
                            return i, j
                else:
                    d = -k
                    if rng.rand() < 0.5:
                        i = int(ones[int(rng.randint(ones.size))])
                        j = i - d
                        if j >= 0 and j < n and h[j] == 0:
                            return i, j
                    else:
                        j = int(zeros[int(rng.randint(zeros.size))])
                        i = j + d
                        if i >= 0 and i < n and h[i] == 1:
                            return i, j

    a = int(ones[int(rng.randint(ones.size))])
    b = int(zeros[int(rng.randint(zeros.size))])
    return a, b


def improve_binary(h_initial, time_limit, rng):
    h = np.asarray(h_initial, dtype=np.int64).copy()
    n = int(len(h))
    if n % 2 != 0:
        n = n - 1
        h = h[:n]

    target = n // 2
    s = int(np.sum(h))
    if s != target:
        if s < target:
            zeros = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
            take = rng.permutation(zeros.size)[: target - s]
            h[zeros[take]] = 1
        else:
            ones = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
            take = rng.permutation(ones.size)[: s - target]
            h[ones[take]] = 0

    C = binary_counts(h)
    best = h.copy()
    best_mx = int(C.max())
    start = time.time()
    no_accept = 0

    while time.time() - start < time_limit:
        cur_mx, cur_cnt, cur_sec = binary_stats(C)
        if cur_mx < best_mx:
            best_mx = cur_mx
            best = h.copy()
        if cur_mx <= 0:
            break

        ones = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
        zeros = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)

        budget = 8 if n >= 500 else 16
        accepted = False

        for _ in range(budget):
            if time.time() - start >= time_limit:
                break
            pair = propose_binary_swap(h, C, cur_mx, rng, ones, zeros)
            if pair is None:
                continue
            a, b = pair
            if a == b:
                continue
            if h[a] != 1 or h[b] != 0:
                if h[a] == 0 and h[b] == 1:
                    a, b = b, a
                else:
                    continue

            apply_swap(C, h, a, b)
            new_mx = int(C.max())

            if new_mx < cur_mx:
                accept = True
            elif new_mx == cur_mx:
                new_cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(C >= new_mx))
                less = C[C < new_mx]
                new_sec = int(less.max()) if less.size else -1
                if (new_cnt < cur_cnt) or (new_cnt == cur_cnt and new_sec < cur_sec):
                    accept = True
                else:
                    accept = rng.rand() < 0.05
            else:
                accept = False

            if accept:
                cur_mx = new_mx
                if new_mx < best_mx:
                    best_mx = new_mx
                    best = h.copy()
                no_accept = 0
                accepted = True
                break
            else:
                revert_swap(C, h, a, b)

        if not accepted:
            no_accept += 1
            if no_accept > 4000:
                h = best.copy()
                C = binary_counts(h)
                ones = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
                zeros = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
                for _ in range(15):
                    a = int(ones[int(rng.randint(ones.size))])
                    b = int(zeros[int(rng.randint(zeros.size))])
                    if a != b:
                        apply_swap(C, h, a, b)
                no_accept = 0

    return best, best_mx


def fractional_polish(h_initial, n, time_limit, rng):
    h = feasible(h_initial, n).astype(float).copy()
    c = task_compute_c5(h)
    best = h.copy()
    best_c = c
    start = time.time()
    stall = 0

    while time.time() - start < time_limit and stall < 180:
        if c < best_c:
            best_c = c
            best = h.copy()
            stall = 0

        corr = corr_direct(h)
        maxv = float(corr.max())
        if not np.isfinite(maxv) or maxv <= 1e-12:
            break

        active = np.flatnonzero(corr >= maxv - 1e-8)
        if active.size == 0:
            active = np.array([int(np.argmax(corr))])

        high = np.flatnonzero(h > 0.55)
        low = np.flatnonzero(h < 0.45)

        improved = False
        for _ in range(25):
            if time.time() - start >= time_limit:
                break

            idx = int(active[int(rng.randint(active.size))])
            d = abs(idx - (n - 1))
            a = None
            b = None

            if d > 0:
                for _ in range(30):
                    if rng.rand() < 0.5:
                        i = int(rng.randint(n - d))
                        u = h[i]
                        v = h[i + d]
                        if u > v + 1e-5:
                            a, b = i, i + d
                            break
                        if v > u + 1e-5:
                            a, b = i + d, i
                            break
                    else:
                        i = d + int(rng.randint(n - d))
                        u = h[i]
                        v = h[i - d]
                        if u > v + 1e-5:
                            a, b = i, i - d
                            break
                        if v > u + 1e-5:
                            a, b = i - d, i
                            break

            if a is None:
                if high.size and low.size:
                    a = int(high[int(rng.randint(high.size))])
                    b = int(low[int(rng.randint(low.size))])

            if a is None or b is None or a == b:
                continue

            x = float(h[a])
            y = float(h[b])
            if x <= y + 1e-6:
                continue

            m = max(0.0, min(x, 1.0 - y))
            if m <= 1e-8:
                continue

            x0 = x
            y0 = y
            best_local = c
            best_move = 0.0

            for r in (0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 0.95):
                mv = r * m
                if mv <= 1e-8:
                    continue
                h[a] = x0 - mv
                h[b] = y0 + mv
                cn = task_compute_c5(h)
                if cn < best_local - 1e-12:
                    best_local = cn
                    best_move = mv

            if best_move > 0.0:
                h[a] = x0 - best_move
                h[b] = y0 + best_move
                c = best_local
                if c < best_c - 1e-12:
                    best_c = c
                    best = h.copy()
                improved = True
                stall = 0
                break
            else:
                h[a] = x0
                h[b] = y0

        if not improved:
            stall += 1

    h = feasible(h, n)
    c = task_compute_c5(h)
    if c < best_c:
        best_c = c
        best = h.copy()
    return best, best_c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start_all = time.time()
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    parent = None
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).ravel()
        if parent.size == 0:
            parent = None
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    best_h = None
    best_c = 1.0
    candidates = []

    if parent is not None:
        hp = feasible(parent, int(parent.size))
        cp = task_compute_c5(hp)
        best_h = hp
        best_c = cp
        candidates.append((hp, cp, int(hp.size)))

    def remaining_time():
        return float(budget_s) - (time.time() - start_all)

    if parent is not None and int(parent.size) % 2 == 0:
        rem = remaining_time()
        if rem > 70:
            pb = to_balanced_binary(parent, int(parent.size), rng)
            lim = min(80.0, rem - 25.0)
            if lim > 10.0:
                hib, mb = improve_binary(pb, lim, rng)
                htmp = hib.astype(float)
                ct = task_compute_c5(htmp)
                if ct < best_c:
                    best_h = htmp
                    best_c = ct
                if ct < 0.39:
                    candidates.append((htmp, ct, int(htmp.size)))

    n_list = [160, 240, 320, 384, 512]
    if parent is not None:
        n_list.insert(0, int(parent.size))

    n_uniq = []
    for n in n_list:
        if n not in n_uniq and n % 2 == 0 and n <= 800:
            n_uniq.append(n)

    for n in n_uniq:
        rem = remaining_time()
        if rem < 70.0:
            break

        if n <= 200:
            attempts = 5
            per = 30.0
        elif n <= 320:
            attempts = 4
            per = 25.0
        else:
            attempts = 3
            per = 22.0

        for att in range(attempts):
            rem = remaining_time()
            if rem < per + 15.0:
                break
            hstart = generate_start(n, att, rng)
            hib, mb = improve_binary(hstart, min(per, rem - 15.0), rng)
            cdirect = 2.0 * mb / float(n)
            if cdirect < best_c + 0.005 or cdirect < 0.385:
                htmp = hib.astype(float)
                ct = task_compute_c5(htmp)
                if ct < best_c:
                    best_h = htmp
                    best_c = ct
                if ct < 0.39:
                    candidates.append((htmp, ct, int(htmp.size)))

    ordered = []
    while candidates:
        bi = -1
        bv = 1e100
        for i in range(len(candidates)):
            if candidates[i][1] < bv:
                bv = candidates[i][1]
                bi = i
        if bi < 0:
            break
        ordered.append(candidates.pop(bi))

    polished = 0
    for idx in range(min(3, len(ordered))):
        rem = remaining_time()
        if rem < 50.0 or polished >= 3:
            break
        h0, _, n0 = ordered[idx]
        if n0 > 800:
            continue
        if n0 <= 320:
            lim = min(130.0, rem - 25.0)
        else:
            lim = min(100.0, rem - 25.0)
        if lim < 15.0:
            break
        hf, cf = fractional_polish(h0.copy(), n0, lim, rng)
        polished += 1
        if cf < best_c:
            best_h = hf
            best_c = cf
        candidates.append((hf, cf, int(hf.size)))

    extra = 0
    while remaining_time() > 55.0 and best_c > 0.3800:
        rem = remaining_time()
        if rem < 45.0:
            break
        n = 512 if rem > 140.0 else 320
        q = 0.990 if (extra % 2 == 0) else 0.975
        hstart = markov_binary(n, q, rng)
        hib, mb = improve_binary(hstart, min(60.0, rem - 25.0), rng)
        htmp = hib.astype(float)
        ct = task_compute_c5(htmp)
        if ct < best_c:
            best_h = htmp
            best_c = ct
        else:
            ct = best_c

        if ct < 0.382 and remaining_time() > 60.0:
            hf, cf = fractional_polish(htmp.copy(), n, min(55.0, remaining_time() - 25.0), rng)
            if cf < best_c:
                best_h = hf
                best_c = cf

        extra += 1
        if extra >= 8:
            break

    if best_h is None:
        nfall = 160
        hfall = markov_binary(nfall, 0.955, rng).astype(float)
        best_h = feasible(hfall, nfall)
        best_c = task_compute_c5(best_h)

    best_h = feasible(best_h, int(len(best_h)))
    best_c = task_compute_c5(best_h)
    return (best_h, best_c, int(len(best_h)))
```
